ParlaMint-AT_2018-06-14-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00031_d8e6149	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Estimated government colleagues! With 1. Austria is entitled to take over the Presidency of the European Union for the third time in July, at a very challenging stage. It will be the last full Presidency of the Council before the elections to the European Parliament. It will be the Presidency of the Council to deal with 300 still open trialogues, and it is the Presidency of the Council, which is likely to face major challenges in the face of Brexit and the negotiations on the multiannual financial framework. A Presidency of the Council is something that not only creates work during the six months in which one works but also a Presidency of the Council is something where there is a great deal of preparatory work to be done. I should therefore like to thank all those who have contributed and continue to do so very much. I can start with the members of the Federal Government, first and foremost with Chancellor Gernot Blümel and also with Foreign Minister Karin Kneissl. However, I would also like to thank all the officials who have been working very intensively here in Austria, but also in the Permanent Representation in Brussels for months to make the Austrian Presidency a success. [[General condolences.]] The Austrian Presidency is taking place at a time of change. They all experience with us that international agreements are currently being concluded – when we look at North Korea. International agreements are announced – when we look into Iran. We are experiencing a more unpredictable situation in the US, still tensions with Russia, and also in the European Union, Brexit has now started a period of change. Since the refugee crisis, there have been increasing tensions in the European Union: The north complains over the south, the west complains over the east. Some Member States have the feeling that there are now first and second-class Member States, and in this respect a major objective of our Presidency is that we want to use our already geographically favourable position in the heart of Europe to achieve this. In the future, the European Union will continue to play a key role in reducing unemployment in the European Union and ensuring that the European Union will continue to become more active in the future. I think it's important to live up to our motto, namely: In diversity, and not in pursuit of the opposite, namely: Separated in equality. – This means a discourse, and that means not only allowing different opinions, but always trying to see that the different approaches that exist in the European Union are discussed at a glance and that dealing with each other is respectful, because only in this way does this project of the European Union have the future. If we give the impression that there are better or worse Europeans, first-class members and second-class members, those who are superior, and those who still need to be educated, then this is at risk to our European Union. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and FPÖ.]] I am coming back from Israel, a state in which, as hardly in any other country in the world, it becomes aware that security is not a matter of course. Unfortunately, however, in recent years God has also shown us in the European Union that security must not be self-evident in Europe, and that it is not God's will that we always live together in stability and security. In this respect, it is our great goal to contribute to the fact that stability, security and prosperity will continue to be a matter of course in the European Union during the Presidency of the Council. As the Federal Government, we have opted for the motto: a Europe that protects, and we therefore want to focus on the issue of security, but also on the issue of prosperity and the safeguarding of our lifestyle and prosperity in Europe. This means, for one thing, that there must be a clear focus on external border protection, because security in the European Union can only exist if not only military security is guaranteed, but also internal security, and in this respect it is also necessary to know who is allowed to migrate and who is not, and it is necessary to ensure that governments decide who is coming to Europe, and that not the tractors are those who make these decisions. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and FPÖ.]] Therefore, in close coordination between the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, the Ministry of the Interior and the Federal Chancellery, we focus on the subject of external border protection. We will be the Summit of Heads of State or Government on 20 October. September in Salzburg dedicated entirely to security, external border protection and the fight against illegal migration. It is our big goal that there will be a strengthening of Frontex, in terms of content, personnel and financial. This means that it will not only increase to 10,000 men until 2027 – as currently foreseen – but will be significantly faster. This means, however, above all, an extension of the Frontex mandate, because only if the officials who work for Frontex are allowed to fight actively against tugpers if they can cooperate with third countries and if they have the opportunity to prevent boats from leaving at all – that is, only if all these possibilities exist – can Frontex officials effectively meet the objective we are pursuing, namely the goal of saving human life, securing our external borders and regaining control in the European Union. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and FPÖ.]] If we are talking about security and a Europe that protects it, then that means not only safety in the physical sense, but also, of course, securing the prosperity we enjoy in Europe. I have many regions as Foreign Minister I have also had to experience that some of us are catching up or even overtakeing, and it is therefore urgently necessary that we as the European Union do everything we can to remain competitive. The Minister for Infrastructure will address this in detail, but it is of course necessary that we in the European Union move forward in digital infrastructure, that we complete the digital internal market, that we eliminate governance rights through the taxation of Internet giants on our continent, and that we invest in science and research in order to continue to be the place in the world where innovation takes place. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and FPÖ.]] Thirdly, In addition to that on classical security and security of prosperity, the focus is of course also – how could it be different! – in our neighborhood. Only if we enjoy stability and security in our neighbourhood will there be stability and security in the European Union. There will therefore be a clear focus on our neighbourhood. The Foreign Minister will focus on the South-East Europe region, with the clear aim of bringing the Western Balkans countries closer not only to the European Union, but also to provide them with support in their European perspective. We thank God just yesterday for the resolution of the name dispute between Greece and Macedonia. We are experiencing great progress when it comes to reforms in Serbia, but also in other countries in that region. It is also our task in Austria to support this region, which is so close to us historically, economically, culturally and humanly, as much as possible in the approach to the European Union. These are, ladies and gentlemen, roughly outline the priorities we set during our Presidency. There are also, of course, many tasks in the areas of competence of all the ministers. I can only assure you that we are looking forward to work, and we are looking forward to the opportunities to develop even more in the European Union than we can already do as a Member State. We are also aware that, in addition to the priorities we have chosen ourselves, of course, major issues such as the conclusion of the Brexit negotiations or the negotiations on the multiannual financial framework, and often also what we cannot foresee, will be drawing this Presidency and shaping this Presidency. I should like to ask all of you for the best possible support during the Presidency of the Council. I believe that it is important that this half-year we take a cross-party lead, for the benefit of Austria, and above all for the benefit of the European Union. Thank you.

We will be able to download mobilely in 5G range 10 gigabits per second. These are speeds that you can't imagine today. But if these networks are attacked, then everything stands: then traffic, the Industry 4.0, the Smart Cities. It is a patriotic act of home love to strengthen Europe. We know that there is no way to overcome the challenges of our time within the borders of a single country. Neither will Germany nor the other major countries succeed. We will only succeed if we work together on the challenges. I would like to point out at this point that we cannot judge everything with the measure of usefulness. Yes, it is true that Austria's national income with EU accession is EUR 50 billion higher than without, but the usefulness is not the only measure we need to assess the European challenges. It is true that Europe – as the Federal Minister is right – is a community of values. I would like to put in place for myself what these values are: It is a commitment to democracy, it is a commitment to the rule of law, it is a commitment to equality, and it is about that we in Europe believe that the dignity of man is indivisible. [[In the case of the SPÖ, the members of the NEOS and the M.A. Zadić.]] When we talk and think about Europe, I believe that Europe is the greatest civilisational progress on our continent. It has happened. If there is an image that we combine with the European agreement, then it is the handiwork of coal and middle edge in Verdun in view of the thousands of graves of people who died during World War II. This is an impressive picture, because the two have shown us that it is possible to cope with centuries of conflicts, chaos, conflicts and rivalries in the common spirit. It was also the middle edge that explained: Nationalism is war! [[Consistency with the SPÖ and the Abg. Zadić.]] I think it is important that we take this into consideration, especially at a time of rise in chauvinism and nationalism in many political issues. They all know the concepts of Italy first, of France first, of perhaps Germany at first – all of these are political approaches that may work in a single country, but if everyone starts to do so, we will not have a stronger, a common Europe at the end, but then all will be losers; that is why we must place nationalism where it belongs, namely, in the past of European history. [[Consistency with the SPÖ and the Abg. Griss.]] We have recently seen a study stating that 45 percent of Austrians are actively committed to the European Union. This is an order of magnitude that is somewhat better than in the immediate past, but cannot be the goal of our wishes. Our prosperity, our security – and the German Chancellor has rightly pointed this out – depends on this European agreement. It is our common task in policy to strengthen and cooperate Europe across the party borders. That is why I am very pleased to accept your offer, Mr Bundeskanzler. From my point of view, however, it is also about maintaining a rational, meaningful dialogue about Europe. Mr Hofer stressed that his idea of Europe is less Europe, but it is more efficient. I believe that we must be very careful to surf the myth of the cucumbering regulation, because we should be very serious about this discussion. In recent weeks we have tried to ask ourselves, because we would like to discuss with you in depth, which is the principle of less Europe, of more efficient Europe, of strengthening subsidiarity. We have tabled a parliamentary question and wanted to know what acts you believe contradict the principle of subsidiarity. Mölzer.]] I believe that we should deal seriously with this, and I also agree that we should solve the major issues in Europe, and there is a chance for the Presidency to do so. We are all together, and you, in particular, have a real chance through the Presidency of the Council to make Europe workable. And we need an actionable Europe! [[In the case of the SPÖ, the members of the NEOS and the M.A. Bißmann.]] I would like to give you a few points that I consider important when we talk about Europe's major questions: We must ensure that solidarity remains intact. One of the points that undermine solidarity is the fact that the tax fraud is organised. I would like you to close the tax routes with all the consequence. [[I should like to congratulate the SPÖ, as well as Mr Noll and Mr Zadić.]] We know that every year 600 billion euros flow from tax havens and are thus deprived of the tax revenue of Europe. It's not acceptable. Our goal must be to combat these tax havens with all the consequence. Our objective must also be to force those who benefit from this big tax fraud and these tax shifts to actually disclose their business. I also have to say that I would like to be more committed to harmonising tax rates. We experience that in our immediate environment – like Hungary – net recipients have reduced their tax rates for corporate profits to 9 percent. There is a spiral in which the only taxpayers remaining in the end will be the workers and employees, the workers, individuals, small and medium-sized enterprises. That is, honestly, not my picture of Europe. If we allow this to happen, we will weaken solidarity. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Secondly, I expect that we are proposing wage and social dumping. Wage and social dumping must not be worthwhile in Europe. Countries with lower social standards cannot benefit if their employees are sent to countries with higher social standards. There is a spiral, at the end of which we are only losers. The restriction of free movement of persons will not help us either, because that is not the right answer. We have taken care of the decision taken by the European Council on the Sends Directive and that it has finally been adopted in the European Parliament three weeks ago. This is crucial progress, but it is not enough. Now it is about – as the ball is with you – that we ensure that the laws that have been passed are actually controlled. We need a European employment agency, best in Austria, and I ask you to support this project. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] In this context, I should like to quote the bishops who have held in their impressive European prayer that it is necessary to build a social Europe. I believe that the social pillar is really crucial, and that is not a question of our approaching the payment of Austrian money to Eastern European countries, but of protecting our own standards. It is also about a rational discussion. The third point that matters to me is the one that we may have forgotten a little. More than ten years ago, the financial crisis, the desert speculation, the demarcated capitalism led us to the brink of the abyss. We have not yet drawn the consequences of what that means. We know we've got to control the speculation. It is important that the financial transaction tax project resumes its journey. It is important that we complete the Banking Union. It is important that we create a European Monetary Fund in order to be able to defend ourselves when history repeats itself. I am not concerned that this is a transfer union, and I would ask you not to approach it, because if there are speculations against whole countries such as Greece or Italy today, then all of us are concerned, then we cannot look away, because in the end we will pay this bill. To be honest, I am a little disappointed about the restraint you have shown in this regard. [[Conjugation with the SPÖ and the Abg. Noll.]] Ladies and gentlemen! I've read, it's about an axis of the Willy. I can only fully support this. I mean, in order to meet these challenges, we should have this axis of willfulness with the Macrons, the Merkel, the Scholz, the Sanchez, is not with those who want to weaken Europe, such as the Orbáns, the Salvinis. We need an effective Europe, and we need to understand that it is our community of fate. [[Congratulations with SPÖ, NEOS and list of fungus.]] I can come to the last sentence. Jacques Delors once formulated: “No one falls in love with a single market”. I would like to add: I think that no one is in love with a single market and not with border security. It is a necessity, but we need a powerful vision and the understanding that true patriotism, true home love is not just red-white-red, but European. Thank you.

It is important for me not to ignore one aspect at this point: the role of Austria in the digital Europe. According to the Digital Economy and Society Index, Austria was in the EU's 10th place in the middle of the EU in 2017. The index assesses digitalisation in the EU Member States in five areas: Connectivity, i.e. technical infrastructure, human capital, digital skills, internet use and the digitalization of the economy. The numbers become interesting when you go into detail. In fact, Austria is cutting off very well in the public service – place five – and in the human capital – place seven. What you can see, however, is that the place ten is quite relative, because in the connectivity we are in place 15, in the digitization of the economy in place 14, in the use of the Internet even in place 20 out of 28. – This is totally unacceptable for a rich and wealthy country like Austria that not all have the internet access they need. [[Congratulations on the Pilz list and on the SPÖ Members.]] I therefore call on the Federal Government to use the Presidency to guide the EU in the right direction and to be strong intermediaries. At the same time, however, we should also use the Presidency to invest in areas that are important for us Austrians and in future for Europe. It's like digital Commissioner Andrus Ansip has always said: Those EU countries, which are the most prominent in the EU-wide digitalisation, are also world-wide, and in the global context also reflect which countries are further behind in the EU. This means to say in his words that all Member States should invest more in order to be able to fully exploit the digital single market. We do not want to have a two-speed Europe in digitization – not a two-speed Europe! It's important that we keep in mind that we take this seriously. There is a need for clear action, and we have to ask ourselves questions such as: How can we use artificial intelligence and robotics for the Austrian industry? How do we ensure that Austrians are familiar with the use of the Internet and also have access to it? However, we also have to ask ourselves these questions at EU level, because, like many others, I do not want Austria at the two speeds, nor do I want to have a two-speed Europe.

As has already been said, it does not make sense to exclude the British more or less, but we want to conduct an open trade with the European State of Great Britain, also in the sense of our citizens and our economic location. There are many other issues, ladies and gentlemen, the Minister has already mentioned them, such as autonomous driving and eco-efficient transport policy. We will discuss these over the next few months. I believe that we can make an essential contribution as a technology carrier, as know-how carriers, especially in terms of efficient transport. I am optimistic that we are putting much in the right direction in this half-year, including as regards mobility in Europe. Thank you.

This is just an example of how one can help to use such tools in teaching, and how it can be individually designed. This is about individualization. What we have also done during the last government period is also to anchor this index of opportunities under the Education Reform Package 2017. We have defined him along six criteria, because until that time school was essentially defined over the number of students and over a special educational needs too short. So how did we define this index of opportunities? – Criteria are the number of pupils – that is clear – the educational range – the priorities of schools – the socio-economic background of parents, the need for support for pupils, their everyday language and regional needs. These are the topics because we all know that schools in rural regions are different from schools in urban regions. A regulation which we have no longer managed in the last legislative period, despite all our efforts, should also define this socio-economic background accordingly; I really ask you to implement it. Pupil Premium – I don't know if you know it – was introduced in 2013 by David Cameron and thus by a conservative government that, as I think, could be a model for you. Schools in England have been going on and on for a long time, so there are also school meals. What has been done is to spend this school meal on children from families who are far-reaching and socio-economically weak, free and free. This is why the authorities know how the composition at which schools is, where there are very many children who come from more remote or socio-economicly weak families. Based on the knowledge from these figures and data, they have caused those schools to simply receive much more resources: Up to 62 percent more resources will be provided to those schools that have special challenges, 62 percent more resources that the directors themselves can allocate. They can decide what their school needs – more social workers, more educators or more psychologists – and what measures they want to take. The second example is the London Challenge, a package of measures focused on this agglomeration. It was launched in 2003 and the starting point was that secondary schools in London have been extremely poorly cut off in the educational characteristics. There were still problems with drugs, violence and mbbing. In Austria, one would say focus schools, although I really don't like to use this term. What was done? – Criteria have been defined on the basis of which schools have been supported accordingly. Learning and school development assistants were provided for these schools. The pedagogicals were trained after qualification and especially for the specific situation, and there were also incentives for educators to teach exactly in these schools. In addition, teach-first teachers added what corresponded to our teacher for Austria, and a peer-learning system was installed after which these schools were connected to highly cutting schools, networked and learned from their experiences. The result was overwhelming. This London Challenge could already be restored in 2011 because the results were so good. The former worst schools were suddenly the best schools in London, and this shows, Mr. Minister, that the use of resources, the use of resources in schools' autonomy must be transferred. This is truly worth it because it really is about opportunities for our children and young people. Take the money in your hands and finance the schools as they really need, with more resources, not only with the 8.8 billion euros – this amount we both know well – but with much more resources, so that exactly those schools get the resources they really need! ParlaMint-AT_2018-06-14-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00031_d8e13053	Madam President. Minister! This was not the case in London, for example, and the conservative government decided in 2003 that they would counter it – and counter-taxing simply means making radical reform plans. In London this was the case with the London Challenge, and this radical step was taken, because there were also problems of discipline and violence. One reads of these in the newspapers in Austria again and again. The reform plan, which was set up in London, consisted of three elements: The comprehensive school autonomy, as mentioned by my colleague Strolz, has been implemented, and you can see that it can be successful if you simply try it; there are certainly radical steps to which you simply have to have the courage. Secondly, intensive parenting was done because it is very important to take parents with them; the parents are very important people for children and young people. The ultimate factor in this example of the Inner City Schools was this student bonus, the Pupil Premium: For each child who comes from disadvantaged conditions, there is a budget bonus for the school. It is very important that there is this budget bonus – in Austria we refer to the opportunities and social index – and some inner-City schools in London have 60 percent more budget than the UK average. And that's important: One must invest in such schools. There is also much more paid for the teachers; this is about the remuneration, but also about the appreciation. It is precisely this kind of investment that has led to the disappearance of the problems of discipline and violence after five years. Most of the problems these schools had could be solved in this way. You have to break that on your tongue! This works with appreciation, investment in teachers, with the involvement of parents and more autonomy. We can learn a lot from that. It is noteworthy that significant progress has already been seen in the key districts of London after five years. Many of these schools simply went better, they were no longer problem schools as before and were replaced in this role by other schools. I am convinced that this is also happening in Austria. We need more courage, we need to invest. So-called focal schools in Austria need additional resources. In particular, support staff are often lacking. It is time to adopt and implement fundamental reforms, to enable fairness of opportunity in the education system and to open up opportunities for all people in the long term, regardless of their origin. But, unfortunately, I do not see any reforms, similar to those in this example. I'm excited about what's still to come. That is why I would like to support Mr Strolz's request, also because I believe that we need more money, more courage and more cooperation with parents and teachers.

The focus of this advisory board is on data protection and consumer protection. The regulatory framework should be a framework that can ensure reasonable action in this area. There is currently a study by a Swiss institute that has assessed cities worldwide on the topic of Fintech development and has established a ranking to what extent these cities are successful in the field of Fintech development. See, in this study, Vienna will be in this field at an international level at a very good 15. # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # # If I may briefly summarise the results of the study: The first place is Singapore, London is still in place in front of us 4, but Tel Aviv in Israel, for example, is in place 20, Tokyo is behind Vienna, Dublin is behind Vienna, Paris is behind Vienna. This is what we have already seen in the international field. a force that is probably not so conscious of us. I think we have to take note of that. I think that's why it's good that you, too, keep this issue up-to-date, also through the request. I can only confirm that we take this very seriously. I have given the requirement that we will define concrete measures from the Fintech Advisory Board by the end of the year and present concrete results in this area. I would also like to say something about concreteisation: The Fintech Advisory Council has already had two meetings, and the third meeting is already taking place at the end of June. So there is really already an active and intensive work. We have three workflows, i.e. three priorities that we look at in this area. The first is also the Regulatory Sandbox requested by you. That is to say, in an experimental framework of a regulatory system – we call it so – the possibility and thus positive conditions are created so that new business models, new forms – not only on the basis of the cryptocurrencies, but also Fintechs or Insurtechs – develop accordingly. I would like to add another important result: This safe space – I consciously use this anglicism – will be available to concession-based companies because it is important to us to ensure the legal framework, but at the same time to drive forward corresponding innovations in Austria. The idea behind it, in this sandbox, is the focus: advice instead of punishment. That is to say, the regulation in this sandbox is to be accompanied from the beginning by the supervisory regulation and it is not to be punished afterwards. The second focus concerns the Cryptoassets and Initial Coin Offerings – that was also addressed by you. I can tell you here that by the end of this year there will be a newsletter of the FMA, in which the findings of the Fintech Advisory Board can already be fixed in a legal adaptation area that is also valid. We will be able to fix this level very consistently and quickly. The third topic: know your customer, KYC abbreviated. This is about ensuring the integrity of this financial market sector. At the same time, we need to ensure that we have a clear, transparent and efficient basis in this area for identifying stakeholders. I think the scandal in the Bitcoin area, which was also raised in Austria in February, has unfortunately contributed to creating an additional dynamic. But I also say: We should not take a case of fraud – no matter what form it happens – to overregulate this area. Unfortunately, there will probably always be deceptive forces of God, no matter how we define the regulatory and legislative ones. The second major area mentioned in the question is the tax issue. It is a question of the framework in which the cryptocurrency is subject to a turnover tax – for example, the topic of exchange. Because only to clarify: Indeed, there was a great media discussion in spring on income tax evasion. In this regard, there is a basis in the 2018 maintenance decree, which makes it clear that the Income Tax Directive has been confirmed, the current legal view remains unchanged and in this field cryptocurrencies are only assessed as capital capital if a interest-bearing investment takes place. That is to say, that is the condition for the tax base. If there is no interest-bearing presumption, then there is only a tax relevance in accordance with § 31 of the Income Tax Act if such recovery is to take place within one year. This means, in summary, that: The taxation of cryptocurrency will continue to concern us both at Austrian and at international level. By means of our driving forces, there is already a working group at EU level, which is working intensively on this, in which representatives of our ministry are also involved at the technical level. In the context of tax relief reform 2020, we will take this issue into account in greater detail. I hope that my summary statements are complementary to the answer to the question, which in all has 44 questions. We have tried to give answers on just under ten pages. I hope that we will have a good basis for the debate, and I conclude in the sense that, as I believe, the House can see that this government and I personally not only take this issue very seriously, but also work very actively to continue to support quickly and quickly what has already developed on a good basis in Austria and in Vienna, so that we can also remain in this technological field. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-06-14-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00031_d8e13536	The next speaker is Mr Lugar. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-06-14-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00031_d8e13558	Mr President. High house! We're talking about blockchain, we're talking about cryptocurrencies and all these things, and that's why we need to find out: What's that all about? Blockchain is something very simple. Many will not know that at all, but you can imagine it this way: For example, if any transactions happen in a village, then these transactions are simply listed; that is, the farmer Huber sells his house to Müller, and that is written on a list. The difference is that this list may have been in the main square at the time and today everyone has a copy of this list at home. – This is blockchain, nothing else: Something is done, a transaction, or an information is processed, and everyone has a copy of it, everyone included in this network. That's a good thing, not a question. This is not a revolution, it is now possible on the basis of the Internet that you do not need a state office, such as a basebook. That is why we are also considering using this blockchain technology in the basic book area, for example in Bolivia, where, through repeated military coups and other problems in the administration and in the government, ownership relationships are changing over and over again, and that is not the right thing. If you have such a list that all have, you can't say the Huber farmer didn't sell the Müller his house, because everyone sees it on his list. This is blockchain, and that's a good thing. You can use that, too. IBM has developed an idea to make products so safe with the help of blockchain technology that it is virtually impossible to fake. This is a good thing, especially with delicate drugs and so on. So, and now we're talking about the shadows: Blockchain is a positive thing, like mobility, like driving – this is something very positive, I think nobody will say that mobility is something bad – but driving cars also causes exhaust gases and is therefore also critical to see. The same is true of blockchain technology. There are growths, and they are called cryptocurrencies. In fact, cryptocurrencies are not a blessing, but rather a curse, because they use the technology – the blockchain that gives security that is comprehensible and transparent – to create a replacement currency, because they simply don't trust the traditional system. It is no coincidence that this currency was invented in 2009, during the financial crisis, because at that time some have considered: How do we create? a currency that can't be manipulated by anyone who can't simply be inflationed if the government or the central bank decides, we're now printing money on devils, and that's transparent? That's what they wanted, and that's what you did with the cryptocurrency. The only problem is that we buy huge disadvantages. It's like I have a stomach ulcer and I'm inventing a better medicine that keeps my pain even more off my body instead of taking care of this stomach ulcer. Cryptocurrencies solve a problem that we would not have had if we had not been so carelessly dealing with our currencies in the past, if we had not allowed one to be assured by financial speculations – tag Lehman-pleite, when one came out of the devil, one with CDS, so-called credit default insurance, – the financial system leads shortly before the collapses. [[Admissibility at the FPÖ]] If we had not had this problem, we would not have needed something like a cryptocurrency that would seem to give us security. But she doesn't, because a cryptocurrency also has huge disadvantages. A cryptocurrency such as bitcoin cannot be multiplied from a certain point on; that is, 21 million bitcoins will exist at the latest in a few years and no more. This is the problem with a currency. Looking back into 1929, one sees that exactly that was the problem. Because of the gold binding, it was not easy to multiply the dollar like that. We know where that happened: in distress and misery. If we actually sacrifice our monetary system because we believe that we can do a little better with a new sanctuary, then we will be in danger of finally being unable to intervene in politics and where one might intervene, as we have seen in 1929. At the moment, with these cryptocurrency currencies, we are heading towards a huge problem. We are already almost 1 000 billion in total capitalization, and if we continue this way, we could have a collapse. That is why we need to step in. I come to the conclusion: Blockchain yes, where it makes sense, but cryptocurrencies belong to closely observed and limited where they represent a danger to all of us.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-06-14-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00031_d8e18029	Madam President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Mr. Federal Minister! Dear spectators at home in front of the screens and here in the High House! Yes, other issues are important to us too, and that is why I am now talking about an important topic, namely accessibility. As a social home party, we are very concerned that people with disabilities can participate freely in daily life. For example, disabled access to ATMs or special adaptations to the specific needs of this group of persons in the field of telephone and transport services, computers or banking services is necessary. Current national requirements for certain products and services differ from one Member State to another and often even within a Member State. This leads to fragmentation, i.e. a fragmentation of the internal market. This makes it very difficult for many companies to provide consumers with accessible products and services. If we make it possible to eliminate and unify existing differences in national legislation, then economic costs will also be avoided. This enables you to invest again in new, innovative accessible products and services. It concerns some 80 million people with disabilities in Europe who benefit from these new directives. By harmonizing legal standards, we are promoting the social participation of people with disabilities within the European Union, and this is a major boost to the economy. Implementation of this draft brings clear structures and legal certainty in this area. Our demands and ideas are in the Council's text of December 2017 and are in line with other European and international legal documents. In order to improve the situation for people with disabilities, the Austrian Federal Government will support the proposal of the Accessibility Directive. - Thank you.

It is an internationally proven means of significantly improving the performance of high-loaded sections of the motorway. For example, the temporary pan strip release is already used successfully in Germany, England, the Netherlands and Switzerland. The advantage of temporary strip release is that the increase in the number of lanes without an expensive and expensive expansion of the roadway can be significantly increased. As a result, the ease, liquidity and safety of transport are significantly increased, as there is a reduction in congestion and as a result a reduction of accidents. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, since I travel with my family very often to Regensburg to visit my parents-in-law, I have already been able to imagine the positive effects of the temporary panstrip release in the Greater Munich area. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Vogl]] There, the temporary pan strip release on the A 99 and on the A 9 is successfully implemented. ParlaMint-AT_2018-06-14-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00031_d8e21201	Mr Eßl, Member of the Commission. — (NL) Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, I should like to thank the honourable Member for his excellent report. - Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-06-14-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00031_d8e21223	Madam President. Federal Minister! When it comes to bringing money to Salzburg, of course, Mr Bacher, we can take the same view and see that we are using approaches that the Federal Minister has perhaps even opened in the previous point today, to say: Of course, we also need something to be done in Salzburg. If we discuss the Risk Goods Promotion Act, it might be interesting to remember briefly what dangerous goods are. These are, therefore, “related to transport in public space substances, preparations” – such as “mixtures, mixes and solutions” – “and articles containing substances of which, because of their nature, physical or chemical characteristics or condition, certain hazards may arise in transport and which are classified as dangerous goods under legislation”. If I may bring a few examples: It's, I think, no one surprised when I say chemicals, liquefied gas, fireworks, gasoline, heating oil: All these are dangerous goods. However, if it goes on and you say that not built-in airbags are dangerous goods because there is a risk of explosion, then one or the other is surprised. But even substances that do not pose a threat in small quantities may be a dangerous substance in large quantities – a lighter, for example, or spray cans. If a truck is loaded with lighters or spray boxes, it is a dangerous transport because there is a danger. There are European regulations, international regulations, several European conventions that determine the circumstances and conditions under which dangerous goods can be transported, and of course there are national regulations. This means that we are in the Risk Goods Promotion Act, which forms the national framework for these regulations. These rules must be adapted regularly to technical developments. This amendment takes account of these amendments as well as Union law. It contains definitions, obligations and stakeholders, exceptions to safeguarding unauthorised access and to the report on road controls to the EU Commission, and this change of law is something that says something about them. Additional changes in the content of the Promotion of Dangerous Goods Act are largely based on experience gained from the application of this Act by relevant economic operators and authorities. They include, for example, clarifications on the definition of vehicles. As the colleague mentioned earlier, this will also restrict the criminal provisions for private recipients of dangerous goods. So these are meaningful changes, we agree with this law!
